   1: """Dynamic stat row generators for resource, construction, and strategic sections.
   2: 
   3: These functions inspect a ship's actual state to generate stat rows on the fly,
   4: rather than using the static JSON-defined layout. This allows the UI to adapt
   5: to different ship configurations (e.g., only showing fuel rows if fuel exists).
   6: """
   7: from __future__ import annotations
   8: 
   9: from typing import Any
  10: from .stat_definitions import StatDefinition
  11: from .stat_getters import (
  12:     fmt_time,
  13:     get_resource_storage, get_resource_generation, get_resource_max_usage,
  14:     get_resource_endurance, get_resource_replenish,
  15: )
  16: 
  17: 
  18: def _label_for(resource_id: str) -> str:
  19:     """Canonical display label for a resource id via ResourceCatalog.
  20: 
  21:     Falls back to the raw id if the catalog can't resolve the name —
  22:     a defensive guard against partial-hydration test contexts that the
  23:     retired hardcoded abbreviation map implicitly tolerated via its
  24:     dict-get default.
  25:     """
  26:     from game.core.resources import ResourceCatalog
  27:     try:
  28:         definition = ResourceCatalog.from_json().get(resource_id)
  29:         if definition is not None:
  30:             return definition.name
  31:     except Exception:  # Intentional broad catch: catalog may be unavailable in some test fixtures.
  32:         pass
  33:     return resource_id
  34: 
  35: 
  36: def _get_constant_consumption(ship, res_name) -> Any:
  37:     """Get constant consumption rate for a resource (excludes activation-based)."""
  38:     from game.simulation.components.abilities.resources import ResourceConsumption
  39:     total = 0
  40:     try:
  41:         for layer in ship.layers.values():
  42:             for comp in layer.components:
  43:                 for ability in comp.ability_instances:
  44:                     if isinstance(ability, ResourceConsumption):
  45:                         if ability.resource_type == res_name and ability.trigger == 'constant':
  46:                             total += ability.amount
  47:     except (TypeError, AttributeError):
  48:         pass
  49:     return total
  50: 
  51: 
  52: def _get_max_endurance(ship, res_name) -> Any:
  53:     """Calculate endurance at max usage rate (constant + all weapons firing)."""
  54:     try:
  55:         capacity = get_resource_storage(ship, res_name)
  56:         max_usage = get_resource_max_usage(ship, res_name)
  57:         if not isinstance(max_usage, (int, float)) or max_usage <= 0:
  58:             return float('inf')
  59:         if not isinstance(capacity, (int, float)):
  60:             return float('inf')
  61:         return capacity / max_usage
  62:     except (TypeError, AttributeError):
  63:         return float('inf')
  64: 
  65: 
  66: def _discover_resources(ship) -> Any:
  67:     """Discover all resource names present on a ship and return sorted.
  68: 
  69:     Sources: resource registry, consumption attributes, generation attributes.
  70:     Returns sorted list: fuel, energy, ammo first, then others alphabetically.
  71:     """
  72:     resource_order = ["fuel", "energy", "ammo"]
  73:     try:
  74:         res_names = set(ship.resources.get_resource_names())
  75:     except (TypeError, AttributeError):
  76:         res_names = set()
  77: 
  78:     for stat_type in ['consumption', 'generation']:
  79:         for res in resource_order:
  80:             try:
  81:                 val = ship.get_resource_stat(res, stat_type)
  82:             except (TypeError, AttributeError):
  83:                 val = 0
  84:             if isinstance(val, (int, float)) and val > 0:
  85:                 res_names.add(res)
  86: 
  87:     res_names = list(res_names)
  88: 
  89:     def sort_key(name) -> Any:
  90:         if name in resource_order:
  91:             return resource_order.index(name)
  92:         return 999
  93: 
  94:     res_names.sort(key=sort_key)
  95:     return res_names
  96: 
  97: 
  98: def _build_resource_rows(ship, resource_name) -> Any:
  99:     """Build conditional stat rows (1-7) for a single resource."""
 100:     try:
 101:         r = ship.resources.get_resource(resource_name)
 102:         max_value = r.max_value if r else 0
 103:         if not isinstance(max_value, (int, float)):
 104:             max_value = 0
 105: 
 106:         const_consumption = _get_constant_consumption(ship, resource_name)
 107:         if not isinstance(const_consumption, (int, float)):
 108:             const_consumption = 0
 109:         max_usage = get_resource_max_usage(ship, resource_name)
 110:         if not isinstance(max_usage, (int, float)):
 111:             max_usage = 0
 112:         generation = get_resource_generation(ship, resource_name)
 113:         if not isinstance(generation, (int, float)):
 114:             generation = 0
 115: 
 116:         if max_value <= 0 and const_consumption <= 0 and max_usage <= 0 and generation <= 0:
 117:             return []
 118:     except (TypeError, AttributeError):
 119:         return []
 120: 
 121:     rows = []
 122:     label_base = resource_name.title()
 123: 
 124:     if max_value > 0:
 125:         rows.append(StatDefinition(
 126:             id=f"max_{resource_name}", label=f"{label_base} Capacity",
 127:             getter=lambda s, n=resource_name: get_resource_storage(s, n),
 128:             formatter="{:.0f}", unit=""
 129:         ))
 130: 
 131:     if generation > 0:
 132:         rows.append(StatDefinition(
 133:             id=f"{resource_name}_gen", label=f"{label_base} Generation",
 134:             getter=lambda s, n=resource_name: get_resource_generation(s, n),
 135:             formatter="{:.1f}", unit="/s"
 136:         ))
 137: 
 138:     if const_consumption > 0:
 139:         rows.append(StatDefinition(
 140:             id=f"{resource_name}_constant", label=f"{label_base} Constant Use",
 141:             getter=lambda s, n=resource_name: _get_constant_consumption(s, n),
 142:             formatter="{:.1f}", unit="/s"
 143:         ))
 144: 
 145:     if max_usage > 0:
 146:         rows.append(StatDefinition(
 147:             id=f"{resource_name}_max_usage", label=f"{label_base} Max Usage",
 148:             getter=lambda s, n=resource_name: get_resource_max_usage(s, n),
 149:             formatter="{:.1f}", unit="/s"
 150:         ))
 151: 
 152:     if max_value > 0 and const_consumption > 0:
 153:         rows.append(StatDefinition(
 154:             id=f"{resource_name}_endurance", label=f"{label_base} Endurance",
 155:             getter=lambda s, n=resource_name: get_resource_endurance(s, n),
 156:             formatter=fmt_time, unit=""
 157:         ))
 158:     elif max_usage > 0:
 159:         rows.append(StatDefinition(
 160:             id=f"{resource_name}_endurance", label=f"{label_base} Endurance",
 161:             getter=lambda s, n=resource_name: _get_max_endurance(s, n),
 162:             formatter=fmt_time, unit=""
 163:         ))
 164: 
 165:     if max_value > 0 and max_usage > const_consumption and const_consumption > 0:
 166:         rows.append(StatDefinition(
 167:             id=f"{resource_name}_max_endurance", label=f"{label_base} Max Endurance",
 168:             getter=lambda s, n=resource_name: _get_max_endurance(s, n),
 169:             formatter=fmt_time, unit=""
 170:         ))
 171: 
 172:     if generation > 0 and const_consumption <= 0 and max_usage <= 0 and max_value > 0:
 173:         rows.append(StatDefinition(
 174:             id=f"{resource_name}_recharge", label=f"{label_base} Recharge",
 175:             getter=lambda s, n=resource_name: get_resource_replenish(s, n),
 176:             formatter=fmt_time, unit=""
 177:         ))
 178: 
 179:     return rows
 180:

 182: def get_logistics_rows(ship) -> Any:
 183:     """Generate stat rows for the Logistics section (dynamic resource rows)."""
 184:     res_names = _discover_resources(ship)
 185:     dynamic_rows = []
 186:     for r_name in res_names:
 187:         dynamic_rows.extend(_build_resource_rows(ship, r_name))
 188:     return dynamic_rows

 211: def _get_strategic_abilities(ship) -> dict:
 212:     """Collect strategic ability data from all components on a ship."""
 213:     harvesters = {}
 214:     storage = {}
 215:     has_planetary_yard = False
 216:     has_space_shipyard = False
 217:     shipyard_info = {}

 223:     except (TypeError, AttributeError):
 224:         layer_items = []

 227:         for comp in getattr(layer, 'components', []):
 228:             for ability in getattr(comp, 'ability_instances', []):
 229:                 cls_name = type(ability).__name__
 230: 
 231:                 if cls_name == 'ResourceHarvesterAbility':
 232:                     res = ability.resource_type
 233:                     rate = ability.base_harvest_rate
 234:                     harvesters[res] = harvesters.get(res, 0.0) + rate
 235:                 elif cls_name == 'LocalStorageAbility':
 236:                     res = ability.resource_type
 237:                     cap = ability.capacity
 238:                     storage[res] = storage.get(res, 0.0) + cap
 239:                 elif cls_name == 'PlanetaryYardAbility':
 240:                     has_planetary_yard = True
 241:                 elif cls_name == 'SpaceShipyardAbility':
 242:                     has_space_shipyard = True
 243:                     shipyard_info = {

 247:                     }
 248:                 elif cls_name == 'StagingYardAbility':
 249:                     staging_capacity += ability.capacity_mass
 250: 
 251:     return {
 252:         'harvesters': harvesters, 'storage': storage,
 253:         'has_planetary_yard': has_planetary_yard,
 254:         'has_space_shipyard': has_space_shipyard,
 255:         'shipyard_info': shipyard_info,
 256:         'staging_capacity': staging_capacity,
 257:     }

       for unknown resources. Out of PROJ-452 scope.

       `RESOURCE_ABBREVS_SHORT` — curated compact-display abbreviations with
       `res[:3]` / `res[0]` fallbacks. Catalog has no abbreviation field;
       catalog-driving would require either a schema extension or accepting
       the slice fallback as canonical. Out of PROJ-452 scope.

  30: 
  31: ## Overview
  32: 
  33: Three independent findings on the resource-catalog boundary plus one container-invariant hardening item that's geographically adjacent. Each finding is the same anti-pattern: **a production surface hardcodes a list of resource IDs or display labels, so adding a new resource to `data/resources.json` silently fails to surface in that consumer until somebody edits the constant.** PROJ-436 Phase 7 retired the worst offender (`RESOURCE_TYPES` constant); this project finishes the sweep on the three surfaces that escaped it.
  34: 
  35: The container-invariant item (DI-005) is included because Phase 1 is already on the resource-boundary file (`game/strategy/data/container.py`); it's a tiny mirror of the existing `Container.add` non-negative guard and the marginal cost of bundling it into a Phase-1 single-PR is lower than spinning a dedicated project for one finding.
  36: 
  37: ## Goals
  38: 
  39: - Remove the last three hardcoded resource lists/labels from production code (`FleetInfo.from_fleet` 8-tuple, `stat_rows_dynamic.LABEL_ABBREV` Ã— 2).
  40: - Drive every UI/DTO resource-iteration loop through `ResourceCatalog.from_json().all_ids()` or `.by_display_group("planetary")` + `ResourceDefinition.name` for display labels.
  41: - Mirror the `Container.add` non-negative guard on `Container.remove` to close DI-2026-05-18-005 (the original primary subject of that DI entry).
  42: - Sweep `stat_rows_dynamic.py` and adjacent UI files for any remaining hardcoded constants in the same anti-pattern.
  43: - Surface no new entries in `AgentCoordination/discovered_issues/log.jsonl` from this project's work unless they are genuine out-of-scope discoveries.
  44:

Add a focused unit test that reproduces the gap before the guard lands.

The IDs they iterate over (`PLANET_RESOURCE_NAMES` at line 177) are already catalog-driven; only the display labels are still hardcoded.
 107: 
 108: Replace the two dicts with a single module-level helper `_label_for(resource_id: str) -> str` that wraps `ResourceCatalog.from_json().get(resource_id).name` (or falls back to the raw `resource_id` if the catalog doesn't have an entry â€” defensive guard against the registry not being fully hydrated during test setup). Call the helper from both `get_construction_rows` and `get_strategic_rows`.
 109: 
 110: Single PR closes both DI-004 (IDs side â€” which is actually already closed at line 177; the DI entry was written before the partial fix) and F-C-015 (labels side â€” the real gap).

 succeeded in 254ms:
   1: """
   2: Resource Catalog and Loading
   3: ============================
   4: 
   5: Provides the unified ResourceCatalog for all resource definitions (planetary
   6: materials and operational consumables), loaded from data/resources.json.
   7: 
   8: Error Handling:
   9:     All loading errors (FileNotFoundError, JSONDecodeError, PermissionError,
  10:     TypeError) are caught and logged, with graceful fallback to default resources.
  11: """
  12: 
  13: import json
  14: import logging
  15: import os
  16: from dataclasses import dataclass
  17: from typing import Dict, List, Optional, Any
  18: 
  19: from game.core.json_utils import load_json_required
  20: from game.core.paths import Paths
  21: 
  22: logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
  23: 
  24: 
  25: # =============================================================================
  26: # Resource Catalog (Unified Resource System)
  27: # =============================================================================
  28: 
  29: @dataclass(frozen=True)
  30: class ResourceDefinition:
  31:     """Immutable definition of a resource type.
  32: 
  33:     Attributes:
  34:         id: Lowercase unique identifier (e.g., "metals", "fuel")
  35:         name: Display name (e.g., "Metals", "Fuel")
  36:         description: Human-readable description
  37:         display_group: Optional UI grouping hint (e.g., "planetary", "operational")
  38:         has_quality: Whether planet deposits of this resource track quality
  39:         mass_per_unit: Mass in tons of one unit of this resource. Drives
  40:             the unified Container substrate (PROJ-436). Defaults to 1.0
  41:             for backward compat with in-memory construction; the
  42:             canonical catalog at `data/resources.json` overrides per
  43:             resource (e.g. metals 0.01, fuel 0.0001, energy 1.0).
  44:     """
  45:     id: str
  46:     name: str
  47:     description: str = ""
  48:     display_group: str = ""
  49:     has_quality: bool = False
  50:     mass_per_unit: float = 1.0
  51: 
  52: 
  53: class ResourceCatalog:
  54:     """Registry of all resource definitions. Loaded once from JSON.
  55: 
  56:     The catalog is the single source of truth for what resources exist
  57:     in the game. Both planetary materials and operational consumables
  58:     are defined here. A resource's behavior (extractable, consumable,
  59:     etc.) is determined by what components and data files reference it,
  60:     not by any category on the resource itself.
  61: 
  62:     Usage:
  63:         # Load from the default data/resources.json
  64:         catalog = ResourceCatalog.from_json()
  65: 
  66:         # Query resources
  67:         catalog.get("metals")           # -> ResourceDefinition or None
  68:         catalog.has("fuel")             # -> True
  69:         catalog.all_ids()               # -> ["metals", "organics", ..., "fuel", ...]
  70:         catalog.by_display_group("planetary")  # -> [ResourceDefinition, ...]
  71: 
  72:         # Build from in-memory data (useful for testing/modding)
  73:         catalog = ResourceCatalog.from_data([{"id": "dilithium", "name": "Dilithium"}])
  74:     """
  75: 
  76:     def __init__(self, definitions: Dict[str, ResourceDefinition]):
  77:         """Initialize with a dict of id -> ResourceDefinition.
  78: 
  79:         Use the factory methods from_json() or from_data() instead of
  80:         calling this directly.
  81:         """
  82:         self._definitions: Dict[str, ResourceDefinition] = dict(definitions)
  83: 
  84:     @classmethod
  85:     def from_json(cls, file_path: str = None) -> 'ResourceCatalog':
  86:         """Load a ResourceCatalog from a JSON file.
  87: 
  88:         Args:
  89:             file_path: Path to the resources JSON file. Supports both
  90:                        absolute paths and paths relative to the project root.
  91: 
  92:         Returns:
  93:             A ResourceCatalog populated with definitions from the file.
  94:             Returns an empty catalog if the file cannot be loaded.
  95:         """
  96:         if file_path is None:
  97:             from game.core.paths import Paths
  98:             file_path = Paths.RESOURCES_FILE
  99:         resolved = _resolve_resource_path(file_path)
 100:         if resolved is None:
 101:             logger.warning(f"Resources file not found at {file_path}, returning empty catalog")
 102:             return cls({})
 103: 
 104:         try:
 105:             data = load_json_required(resolved)
 106:             resource_list = data.get('resources', [])
 107:             return cls.from_data(resource_list)
 108:         except (FileNotFoundError, json.JSONDecodeError, PermissionError,
 109:                 OSError, TypeError, AttributeError) as e:
 110:             logger.warning(f"Failed to load resources from {resolved}: {e}")
 111:             return cls({})
 112: 
 113:     @classmethod
 114:     def from_data(cls, resource_list: List[Dict[str, Any]]) -> 'ResourceCatalog':
 115:         """Build a ResourceCatalog from a list of resource dictionaries.
 116: 
 117:         Each dict should have at minimum an 'id' and 'name' key.
 118:         Missing optional fields get sensible defaults.
 119: 
 120:         Args:
 121:             resource_list: List of resource definition dicts.
 122: 
 123:         Returns:
 124:             A ResourceCatalog populated with the given definitions.
 125:         """
 126:         definitions: Dict[str, ResourceDefinition] = {}
 127:         for entry in resource_list:
 128:             res_id = entry.get('id')
 129:             if not res_id:
 130:                 continue
 131:             definitions[res_id] = ResourceDefinition(
 132:                 id=res_id,
 133:                 name=entry.get('name', res_id),
 134:                 description=entry.get('description', ''),
 135:                 display_group=entry.get('display_group', ''),
 136:                 has_quality=entry.get('has_quality', False),
 137:                 mass_per_unit=float(entry.get('mass_per_unit', 1.0)),
 138:             )
 139:         return cls(definitions)
 140:

   2: Build Queue Helpers - Pure formatting functions for build queue display.

   5: """
   6: from functools import lru_cache
   7: 
   8: from game.core.resources import ResourceCatalog
   9: 
  10: 
  11: @lru_cache(maxsize=1)
  12: def _get_planetary_ids() -> tuple[str, ...]:
  13:     """PROJ-393: lazy-load planetary resource IDs (was a module-level
  14:     ``ResourceCatalog.from_json()`` call that ran at import time, breaking
  15:     the docs/02_PATTERNS.md Pattern 12 contract). The cached tuple is
  16:     cheap to iterate; tests can call ``_get_planetary_ids.cache_clear()``."""
  17:     return tuple(d.id for d in ResourceCatalog.from_json().by_display_group("planetary"))
  18: 
  19: # Resource abbreviations for compact UI display
  20: RESOURCE_ABBREVS = {
  21:     "metals": "Met",
  22:     "organics": "Org",
  23:     "vapors": "Vap",
  24:     "radioactives": "Rad",

  27:

  31:     "organics": "O",
  32:     "vapors": "V",

  35: }
  36: 
  37: 
  38: def format_empire_resources(empire) -> str:
  39:     """Format empire resource pool for display in build queue bottom bar.
  40: 
  41:     Args:
  42:         empire: Empire instance with resource_pool.
  43: 
  44:     Returns:
  45:         Formatted string like "Met: 500/1000  Org: 200/500  Vap: 0"
  46:     """
  47:     parts = []
  48:     for res in _get_planetary_ids():
  49:         current = empire.resource_pool.get(res, 0.0)
  50:         cap = empire.max_storage.get(res, 0.0)
  51:         abbr = RESOURCE_ABBREVS.get(res, res[:3])
  52:         if cap > 0:
  53:             parts.append(f"{abbr}: {int(current)}/{int(cap)}")
  54:         elif current > 0:
  55:             parts.append(f"{abbr}: {int(current)}")
  56:     return "  |  ".join(parts) if parts else "No resources"
  57: 
  58: 
  59: def calculate_per_turn_spend(
  60:     queue_item: dict, build_rate: dict
  61: ) -> dict:
  62:     """Calculate proportional per-turn resource spend for a single queue item.
  63: 
  64:     Uses the limiting-resource formula: find the resource that takes the
  65:     longest to complete (max remaining/rate), then each resource spends
  66:     remaining/limiting_turns per turn. All resources progress at the same
  67:     fractional rate toward completion (spend ratio matches cost ratio).
  68: 
  69:     Args:
  70:         queue_item: Dict with 'total_cost' and 'resources_consumed' keys.
  71:         build_rate: Dict mapping resource name to production rate per turn.
  72: 
  73:     Returns:
  74:         Dict mapping resource name to per-turn spend amount. Returns empty
  75:         dict if no remaining costs. Returns all zeros if any required
  76:         resource has zero production rate.
  77:     """
  78:     total_cost = queue_item.get("total_cost", {})
  79:     resources_consumed = queue_item.get("resources_consumed", {})
  80: 
  81:     if not total_cost:
  82:         return {}
  83: 
  84:     # Calculate remaining cost per resource
  85:     remaining = {}
  86:     for res, amount in total_cost.items():
  87:         rem = max(0.0, amount - resources_consumed.get(res, 0.0))
  88:         if rem > 0:
  89:             remaining[res] = rem
  90:

  16: - LayerType: Runtime - iterates ship layers for layer status display

  18: from __future__ import annotations

  21: from pygame_gui.elements import UILabel, UIScrollingContainer, UITextBox

  31: 
  32: class StatRow:
  33:     """Helper class to manage a single statistic row (Label | Value | Unit) with caching."""
  34: 
  35:     def __init__(self, key: str, label_text: str, manager, container, x: int, y: int, width: int):
  36:         """
  37:         Initialize a stat row with label, value, and unit elements.
  38: 
  39:         Args:
  40:             key: Unique identifier for this row
  41:             label_text: Display text for the label
  42:             manager: pygame_gui UIManager
  43:             container: Parent UI container
  44:             x: X position within container
  45:             y: Y position within container
  46:             width: Total width for the row
  47:         """
  48:         self.key = key
  49:         # Layout: Label 50%, Value 30%, Unit 20%
  50:         lbl_w = int(width * 0.50)
  51:         val_w = int(width * 0.30)
  52:         unit_w = width - lbl_w - val_w
  53: 
  54:         self.label = UILabel(pygame.Rect(x, y, lbl_w, 20), f"{label_text}:",
  55:                            manager=manager, container=container, object_id="#stat_label")
  56:         self.value = UILabel(pygame.Rect(x + lbl_w, y, val_w, 20), "--",
  57:                            manager=manager, container=container, object_id="#stat_value")
  58:         self.unit = UILabel(pygame.Rect(x + lbl_w + val_w, y, unit_w, 20), "",
  59:                           manager=manager, container=container, object_id="#stat_unit")
  60: 
  61:         self._last_val: Optional[str] = None
  62:         self._last_unit: Optional[str] = None
  63:         self._visible: bool = True
  64: 
  65:     def update(self, val_text: str, unit_text: str = "") -> None:
  66:         """
  67:         Update the value and unit text with caching to avoid unnecessary UI updates.
  68: 
  69:         Args:
  70:             val_text: New value text to display
  71:             unit_text: New unit text to display
  72:         """
  73:         if self._last_val != val_text:
  74:             self.value.set_text(val_text)
  75:             self._last_val = val_text
  76: 
  77:         if self._last_unit != unit_text:
  78:             self.unit.set_text(unit_text)
  79:             self._last_unit = unit_text
  80: 
  81:     def set_visible(self, visible: bool) -> None:
  82:         """
  83:         Show or hide the entire row.
  84: 
  85:         Args:
  86:             visible: Whether the row should be visible
  87:         """
  88:         if self._visible == visible:
  89:             return
  90: 
  91:         if visible:
  92:             self.label.show()
  93:             self.value.show()
  94:             self.unit.show()
  95:         else:
  96:             self.label.hide()
  97:             self.value.hide()
  98:             self.unit.hide()
  99:         self._visible = visible

 102: class DesignStatsPanel:
 103:     """
 104:     Shared stats panel widget for displaying ship statistics.
 105: 
 106:     This panel provides a two-column layout showing all ship stats including:
 107:     - Main Systems, Maneuvering, Shields, Armor
 108:     - Layer distribution (dynamic slots)
 109:     - Targeting, Logistics (dynamic per resources)
 110:     - Crew Logistics, Fighter Support
 111:     - Build Cost (construction resources)
 112:     - Optional: Requirements and Recommendations sections
 113: 
 114:     Usage:
 115:         panel = DesignStatsPanel(manager, rect, container, ship=my_ship, show_requirements=True)
 116:         # Later:
 117:         if panel.needs_rebuild(new_ship):
 118:             panel.rebuild(new_ship)
 119:         else:
 120:             panel.update_stats(new_ship)
 121:     """
 122: 
 123:     def __init__(
 124:         self,
 125:         manager,
 126:         rect: pygame.Rect,
 127:         container,
 128:         ship: Optional["Ship"] = None,
 129:         show_requirements: bool = False
 130:     ):
 131:         """
 132:         Initialize the design stats panel.

 138:             ship: Optional Ship object to initialize with
 139:             show_requirements: Whether to show Requirements/Recommendations sections
 140:         """

 142:         self.rect = rect

 177:             self.stats_scroll = None
 178: 
 179:         # Clear existing state
 180:         self.rows_map = {}

+## Summary
+
+The three production edits on `group-c` match the documented Phase 1-3 scopes and I did not find a production-code regression that should block merge. Phase checklist closure is present in the per-phase files, DI-003/004/005 are resolved in `AgentCoordination/discovered_issues/log.jsonl:5-7`, F-C-015 closure is recorded in `Projects/active_projects/PROJ-452/decisions.md:10-16`, and the sharded baseline artifact records `23376/23376` in `AgentCoordination/generated/test_baseline.json:2-9` with the per-install receipt tied to the Phase 3 SHA in `AgentCoordination/generated/test_baseline/by_install/304cdac3c17d4dd6b885c50bf357d6cd.json:2-11`. I did not rerun tests because `allow_tests=false`, so the focused-pytest and sharded claims here are artifact-backed rather than re-executed in this consult. The one concrete merge-prep issue is project-artifact drift: `Projects/active_projects/PROJ-452/plan.md` still reflects a pre-Phase-4-gate / pre-audit state even though the supporting artifacts say those gates are complete.
+
+## Verified issues
+
+- `Projects/active_projects/PROJ-452/plan.md` is stale relative to the completed Phase 4 and audit handoff. `Projects/active_projects/PROJ-452/phase_4_checklist.md:96-103` marks the sweep, decisions entry, tests, sharded gate, validation, and Current State update complete, but `Projects/active_projects/PROJ-452/plan.md:25-27` still says the audit is pending and the next action is to run the Phase 4 sharded gate and commit/push Phase 4. The top-level verification list is also still unchecked at `Projects/active_projects/PROJ-452/plan.md:155-163` even though the evidence exists elsewhere: all four phase completion checklists are fully checked (`phase_1_checklist.md:55-62`, `phase_2_checklist.md:102-109`, `phase_3_checklist.md:82-91`, `phase_4_checklist.md:96-103`), DI-003/004/005 are resolved (`AgentCoordination/discovered_issues/log.jsonl:5-7`), F-C-015 closure is recorded (`Projects/active_projects/PROJ-452/decisions.md:10-16`), and the sharded baseline is green (`AgentCoordination/generated/test_baseline.json:2-9`). This is the only issue I found that still needs cleanup before merge.
+
+## False positives
+
+- I reject “hidden production drift outside the documented code-change scope.” The project scope is explicitly limited to `container.py`, `fleet_dto.py`, `stat_rows_dynamic.py`, and the matching tests in `Projects/active_projects/PROJ-452/plan.md:47-52`. The actual production diffs stay narrow: Phase 1 adds only the two negative-quantity guards in `game/strategy/data/container.py:225-249`; Phase 2 adds one `ResourceCatalog` import and swaps the two tuples for catalog iteration in `game/strategy/facade/dto/fleet_dto.py:231-237`; Phase 3 adds `_label_for()` and replaces exactly three label call sites in `game/ui/screens/builder/stat_rows_dynamic.py:18-33`, `:191-205`, and `:260-283`.
+
+- I reject “Phase 2 introduced a live order-sensitivity regression.” `ResourceCatalog.all_ids()` explicitly preserves JSON order in `game/core/resources.py:149-151`, the current catalog order in `data/resources.json:4-65` matches the legacy 8-resource tuple order, and catalog-order DTO emission is already an established pattern in `game/strategy/facade/dto/planet_dto.py:44-56` and `game/strategy/facade/dto/empire_dto.py:104-109`. Test coverage is also sufficient for the changed behavior: `tests/unit/strategy/facade/test_fleet_dto.py:590-607` is the real regression catcher for new catalog IDs, while `:577-588` is characterization rather than the sole proof.
+
+- I reject “the remaining Phase 4 candidates are the same silent-loss anti-pattern as DI-003/004.” `_discover_resources()` uses `resource_order = ["fuel", "energy", "ammo"]` only as preferred ordering, with unknown resources still retained and sorted after discovery in `game/ui/screens/builder/stat_rows_dynamic.py:66-95`. `RESOURCE_ABBREVS` / `RESOURCE_ABBREVS_SHORT` in `game/ui/screens/build_queue_helpers.py:19-35` also have non-silent fallbacks at `:51-56`. I agree with the project’s classification in `Projects/active_projects/PROJ-452/decisions.md:12-16`.
+
+- I reject “the `_label_for()` fallback can silently drop rows.” The helper only affects label text and falls back to the raw resource id on failure in `game/ui/screens/builder/stat_rows_dynamic.py:18-33`; the construction/strategic row builders still emit rows from catalog IDs or discovered abilities at `:191-205` and `:260-283`. The visible label behavior is directly locked by `tests/unit/ui/screens/builder/test_stat_rows_dynamic.py:302-347`.
+
+## Out-of-scope items observed
+
+- The auxiliary allowlist commit (`bef489111`) is real, but it is non-production and separately documented in `Projects/active_projects/PROJ-452/plan.md:175-177`. The diff is confined to `Tools/lint_test_files_allowlist.txt` and does not change PROJ-452 production behavior.
+
+- The main non-obvious risk in the production diff is performance, not correctness: `_label_for()` calls `ResourceCatalog.from_json()` on every label lookup in `game/ui/screens/builder/stat_rows_dynamic.py:18-33`, and `FleetInfo.from_fleet()` now calls it twice per DTO build in `game/strategy/facade/dto/fleet_dto.py:231-237`. `ResourceCatalog.from_json()` does real JSON loading in `game/core/resources.py:96-107`. That said, these dynamic row builders feed layout rebuilds rather than per-frame updates (`game/ui/panels/design_stats_panel.py:461-506`), so I would treat this as future cleanup/profiling material, not as a discovered-issue log candidate from this audit.
+
+- I do not see a new diff-local production finding that clearly merits `/claude-di-log`. The unresolved item here is the stale project bookkeeping in `plan.md`, which should be fixed directly in PROJ-452 rather than logged as a separate discovered issue.
+
+## Final verdict
+
+Extra phases needed. The production code changes are mergeable, but I would not call the project fully closed until `Projects/active_projects/PROJ-452/plan.md` is reconciled with the already-complete phase artifacts: update Current State / Next Action and tick the top-level verification boxes that are already supported by `log.jsonl`, `decisions.md`, the phase checklists, and the sharded baseline receipt. After that bookkeeping cleanup, I do not see a production-code reason to hold the merge.

+- I reject “Phase 2 introduced a live order-sensitivity regression.” `ResourceCatalog.all_ids()` explicitly preserves JSON order in `game/core/resources.py:149-151`, the current catalog order in `data/resources.json:4-65` matches the legacy 8-resource tuple order, and catalog-order DTO emission is already an established pattern in `game/strategy/facade/dto/planet_dto.py:44-56` and `game/strategy/facade/dto/empire_dto.py:104-109`. Test coverage is also sufficient for the changed behavior: `tests/unit/strategy/facade/test_fleet_dto.py:590-607` is the real regression catcher for new catalog IDs, while `:577-588` is characterization rather than the sole proof.
+
+- I reject “the remaining Phase 4 candidates are the same silent-loss anti-pattern as DI-003/004.” `_discover_resources()` uses `resource_order = ["fuel", "energy", "ammo"]` only as preferred ordering, with unknown resources still retained and sorted after discovery in `game/ui/screens/builder/stat_rows_dynamic.py:66-95`. `RESOURCE_ABBREVS` / `RESOURCE_ABBREVS_SHORT` in `game/ui/screens/build_queue_helpers.py:19-35` also have non-silent fallbacks at `:51-56`. I agree with the project’s classification in `Projects/active_projects/PROJ-452/decisions.md:12-16`.
+
+- I reject “the `_label_for()` fallback can silently drop rows.” The helper only affects label text and falls back to the raw resource id on failure in `game/ui/screens/builder/stat_rows_dynamic.py:18-33`; the construction/strategic row builders still emit rows from catalog IDs or discovered abilities at `:191-205` and `:260-283`. The visible label behavior is directly locked by `tests/unit/ui/screens/builder/test_stat_rows_dynamic.py:302-347`.
+
+## Out-of-scope items observed
+
+- The auxiliary allowlist commit (`bef489111`) is real, but it is non-production and separately documented in `Projects/active_projects/PROJ-452/plan.md:175-177`. The diff is confined to `Tools/lint_test_files_allowlist.txt` and does not change PROJ-452 production behavior.
+
+- The main non-obvious risk in the production diff is performance, not correctness: `_label_for()` calls `ResourceCatalog.from_json()` on every label lookup in `game/ui/screens/builder/stat_rows_dynamic.py:18-33`, and `FleetInfo.from_fleet()` now calls it twice per DTO build in `game/strategy/facade/dto/fleet_dto.py:231-237`. `ResourceCatalog.from_json()` does real JSON loading in `game/core/resources.py:96-107`. That said, these dynamic row builders feed layout rebuilds rather than per-frame updates (`game/ui/panels/design_stats_panel.py:461-506`), so I would treat this as future cleanup/profiling material, not as a discovered-issue log candidate from this audit.
+
+- I do not see a new diff-local production finding that clearly merits `/claude-di-log`. The unresolved item here is the stale project bookkeeping in `plan.md`, which should be fixed directly in PROJ-452 rather than logged as a separate discovered issue.
+
+## Final verdict
+
+Extra phases needed. The production code changes are mergeable, but I would not call the project fully closed until `Projects/active_projects/PROJ-452/plan.md` is reconciled with the already-complete phase artifacts: update Current State / Next Action and tick the top-level verification boxes that are already supported by `log.jsonl`, `decisions.md`, the phase checklists, and the sharded baseline receipt. After that bookkeeping cleanup, I do not see a production-code reason to hold the merge.

+## Summary
+
+The three production edits on `group-c` match the documented Phase 1-3 scopes and I did not find a production-code regression that should block merge. Phase checklist closure is present in the per-phase files, DI-003/004/005 are resolved in `AgentCoordination/discovered_issues/log.jsonl:5-7`, F-C-015 closure is recorded in `Projects/active_projects/PROJ-452/decisions.md:10-16`, and the sharded baseline artifact records `23376/23376` in `AgentCoordination/generated/test_baseline.json:2-9` with the per-install receipt tied to the Phase 3 SHA in `AgentCoordination/generated/test_baseline/by_install/304cdac3c17d4dd6b885c50bf357d6cd.json:2-11`. I did not rerun tests because `allow_tests=false`, so the focused-pytest and sharded claims here are artifact-backed rather than re-executed in this consult. The one concrete merge-prep issue is project-artifact drift: `Projects/active_projects/PROJ-452/plan.md` still reflects a pre-Phase-4-gate / pre-audit state even though the supporting artifacts say those gates are complete.
+
+## Verified issues
+
+- `Projects/active_projects/PROJ-452/plan.md` is stale relative to the completed Phase 4 and audit handoff. `Projects/active_projects/PROJ-452/phase_4_checklist.md:96-103` marks the sweep, decisions entry, tests, sharded gate, validation, and Current State update complete, but `Projects/active_projects/PROJ-452/plan.md:25-27` still says the audit is pending and the next action is to run the Phase 4 sharded gate and commit/push Phase 4. The top-level verification list is also still unchecked at `Projects/active_projects/PROJ-452/plan.md:155-163` even though the evidence exists elsewhere: all four phase completion checklists are fully checked (`phase_1_checklist.md:55-62`, `phase_2_checklist.md:102-109`, `phase_3_checklist.md:82-91`, `phase_4_checklist.md:96-103`), DI-003/004/005 are resolved (`AgentCoordination/discovered_issues/log.jsonl:5-7`), F-C-015 closure is recorded (`Projects/active_projects/PROJ-452/decisions.md:10-16`), and the sharded baseline is green (`AgentCoordination/generated/test_baseline.json:2-9`). This is the only issue I found that still needs cleanup before merge.
+
+## False positives
+
+- I reject “hidden production drift outside the documented code-change scope.” The project scope is explicitly limited to `container.py`, `fleet_dto.py`, `stat_rows_dynamic.py`, and the matching tests in `Projects/active_projects/PROJ-452/plan.md:47-52`. The actual production diffs stay narrow: Phase 1 adds only the two negative-quantity guards in `game/strategy/data/container.py:225-249`; Phase 2 adds one `ResourceCatalog` import and swaps the two tuples for catalog iteration in `game/strategy/facade/dto/fleet_dto.py:231-237`; Phase 3 adds `_label_for()` and replaces exactly three label call sites in `game/ui/screens/builder/stat_rows_dynamic.py:18-33`, `:191-205`, and `:260-283`.
+
+- I reject “Phase 2 introduced a live order-sensitivity regression.” `ResourceCatalog.all_ids()` explicitly preserves JSON order in `game/core/resources.py:149-151`, the current catalog order in `data/resources.json:4-65` matches the legacy 8-resource tuple order, and catalog-order DTO emission is already an established pattern in `game/strategy/facade/dto/planet_dto.py:44-56` and `game/strategy/facade/dto/empire_dto.py:104-109`. Test coverage is also sufficient for the changed behavior: `tests/unit/strategy/facade/test_fleet_dto.py:590-607` is the real regression catcher for new catalog IDs, while `:577-588` is characterization rather than the sole proof.
+
+- I reject “the remaining Phase 4 candidates are the same silent-loss anti-pattern as DI-003/004.” `_discover_resources()` uses `resource_order = ["fuel", "energy", "ammo"]` only as preferred ordering, with unknown resources still retained and sorted after discovery in `game/ui/screens/builder/stat_rows_dynamic.py:66-95`. `RESOURCE_ABBREVS` / `RESOURCE_ABBREVS_SHORT` in `game/ui/screens/build_queue_helpers.py:19-35` also have non-silent fallbacks at `:51-56`. I agree with the project’s classification in `Projects/active_projects/PROJ-452/decisions.md:12-16`.
+
+- I reject “the `_label_for()` fallback can silently drop rows.” The helper only affects label text and falls back to the raw resource id on failure in `game/ui/screens/builder/stat_rows_dynamic.py:18-33`; the construction/strategic row builders still emit rows from catalog IDs or discovered abilities at `:191-205` and `:260-283`. The visible label behavior is directly locked by `tests/unit/ui/screens/builder/test_stat_rows_dynamic.py:302-347`.
+
+## Out-of-scope items observed
+
+- The auxiliary allowlist commit (`bef489111`) is real, but it is non-production and separately documented in `Projects/active_projects/PROJ-452/plan.md:175-177`. The diff is confined to `Tools/lint_test_files_allowlist.txt` and does not change PROJ-452 production behavior.
+
+- The main non-obvious risk in the production diff is performance, not correctness: `_label_for()` calls `ResourceCatalog.from_json()` on every label lookup in `game/ui/screens/builder/stat_rows_dynamic.py:18-33`, and `FleetInfo.from_fleet()` now calls it twice per DTO build in `game/strategy/facade/dto/fleet_dto.py:231-237`. `ResourceCatalog.from_json()` does real JSON loading in `game/core/resources.py:96-107`. That said, these dynamic row builders feed layout rebuilds rather than per-frame updates (`game/ui/panels/design_stats_panel.py:461-506`), so I would treat this as future cleanup/profiling material, not as a discovered-issue log candidate from this audit.
+
+- I do not see a new diff-local production finding that clearly merits `/claude-di-log`. The unresolved item here is the stale project bookkeeping in `plan.md`, which should be fixed directly in PROJ-452 rather than logged as a separate discovered issue.
+
+## Final verdict
+
+Extra phases needed. The production code changes are mergeable, but I would not call the project fully closed until `Projects/active_projects/PROJ-452/plan.md` is reconciled with the already-complete phase artifacts: update Current State / Next Action and tick the top-level verification boxes that are already supported by `log.jsonl`, `decisions.md`, the phase checklists, and the sharded baseline receipt. After that bookkeeping cleanup, I do not see a production-code reason to hold the merge.

The verdict in the response is `extra phases needed`, limited to reconciling that plan file with the already-complete phase artifacts.
